  (`fragment_asm.h`: `opcode:4; reg:6`), so this is not an overlap with the mul
  fields in the encoder, and the two shapes in 53.2 show the same index is not a
  row either.

### 53.4 What this means for the compiler's cube-coordinate word

- Confirmed by measurement: `0x28689402 0x00000000` cannot be computing
  "r0 = r2 * (1/r10), r1 = r2 * (1/r10)" on this hardware. The word with r10 as
  the sfu source returns `0xF1000000` where its own disassembly predicts
  `0xEF00F300`, and changing the uniform it is supposed to scale changes
  nothing.
- Inferred: the cgc uniform cube-coordinate path needs a row that the fragment
  hardware does not provide as a row register, or needs driver state this
  harness never sets. The row it names is not one the shader can write, and the
  unit's own state behind that index changes with the shape of the surrounding
  program, so no shader-side value can be the intended operand. **Retracted by
  note 54.** The index is not a missing row: it is the major-axis selector over
  r0, r1 and r2. What note 54 leaves unresolved is the word's two multipliers.
- Unknown: whether the real blob's driver writes anything into that index
  before the word runs. The harness drives its own fragment state, so a
  driver-side initialisation outside the shader would not appear here.
- Unchanged from notes 51 and 52: the TEX word itself has no cube field in
  these dumps and the harness has no cube binding path, so which face gets
  sampled is still unmeasured.

### 53.5 Method notes

- An ALU read of a row written earlier in the same packet returns 0
  (`mfu_alu_rows.in`: `echo-r2` and `echo-r3` both `0xF7000000` with the marker
  intact, and `echo-r4`..`echo-r12` `0xF700FF00`), so every row read here
  crosses a batch boundary.
- `sfu: nop` needs its row operand (`nop r0`); without it the assembler reports
  `fs: line 21: syntax error`. A bare `mul1: NOP` is not an MFU function either
  and fails the same way.
- Storing in the same packet as the multipliers needs no correction: the
  2-batch `mfu_uniform_mul` manifest and the 3-batch shape return the same code
  for the same operands (`product-nosfu` `0xFB00F300` is 0.75 and 1.5,
  `word-nosfu` `0xEF00F300` is 0.75 and 0.5; only the seeded second row differs
  between those two programs), so no read-after-write correction is applied
  anywhere in this note.

### 53.6 Runs

`calib-store-20261125T064321Z` (12 variants, 28 runs),
`mfu-r10-seed-20261125T063935Z` (6, 22),
`mfu-alu-rows-20261124T234441Z` (6, 22),
`mfu-row-src-20261124T234542Z` (8, 28),
`mfu-src-sweep-20261125T063356Z` (22, 70),
`mfu-src-map-20261125T063712Z` (18, 58),
`mfu-high-group-20261125T063822Z` (5, 19),
`mul-probe-20261125T064447Z` (5, 19),
`mfu-uniform-mul-20261125T063940Z` (4, 16),
`mfu-uniform-mul3-20261125T064610Z` (5, 19),
`mfu-word-x-20261125T064727Z` (5, 19),
`r10-clean-20261125T064818Z` (5, 19). Every manifest carries the two standard
controls; no run in this note failed or timed out.

## 54. The special SFU source is the absolute maximum of r0, r1 and r2 - the cube major-axis reciprocal

Note 53 found that the SFU source indices at or above 8 do not name rows and that
the compiler's uniform cube word behaves unexpectedly with `sfu: rcp r10`. This
note tests the reading those observations suggested: that the special index
selects the largest absolute component of a cube-map direction, so
`sfu: rcp r10` computes `1 / max(|x|, |y|, |z|)`, the major-axis reciprocal a
cube-map projection needs.

Verdict: **the maximum-absolute-component reading is confirmed** for positive and
negative inputs, for r0, r1 and r2, with the sign dropped, and with r3 and rows
above it excluded. The multiplier interaction in the compiler's own word stays
**unresolved**: with an ordinary row as the sfu source the same multiplies are
exact, with an index at or above 8 they are not, and this note does not find the
rule behind that.

Measured on the Toshiba AC100 (`7.2.0-gd1efa725955b`, Tegra 20, AR20 fragment
path) with `~/build/grate-exp`, three repeats per variant, controls before and
after every manifest (`immediate-operand` `0x000000FF`, `varying-pipeline`
`0xFF01FF00`). Fifteen manifests, 144 variants, 492 runs, no failures. All
inputs are loaded through ALU `.lh` writes, so every row holds fp20, and the raw
words, generated `.asm`, stdout and stderr are under `characterization/` as
named in 54.9.

### 54.1 The probe shape, and why the result is copied twice

`tests/grate/asm/mfu_r10_xyz.in`: batch 0 seeds r0, r1, r2 and r3 from four
fragment constants, batch 1 runs `sfu: rcp <index>` and copies the SFU result
into r0 and r1 through both multipliers (`mul0: r0, sfu, #1` and
`mul1: r1, sfu, #1`), and batch 2 stores the r0/r1 pair. Both store lanes then
carry the same SFU result and no seeded row is read back, which matters because
the special source disturbs the row it selects (54.6). The readback is the store
byte calibrated in 53.1: `code = 0xF7 + 8*log2(value)`, so 0.5 is `EF`, 0.25 is
`E7`, 0.125 is `DF`, 0.0625 is `D7`, anything at or above 2.0 clips to `FF`, and
`00` is zero.

The store pair is also constrained: the assembler accepts only `r0,r1` or
`r2,r3` (`DW source registers should be either "r0,r1" or "r2,r3"`), which is
why every probe here stores one of those two pairs.

### 54.2 Which components participate

`hyp-a-20261125T072948Z`, nine permutations, with the expected result under each
reading (H1 = `1/max(|r0|,|r1|,|r2|)`, H4 = r2 ignored):

| r0 | r1 | r2 | word | result | H1 | H4 |
|--:|--:|--:|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| 8 | 4 | 2 | `0xDF00DF00` | 0.125 | 0.125 | 0.125 |
| 4 | 8 | 2 | `0xDF00DF00` | 0.125 | 0.125 | 0.125 |
| 4 | 2 | 8 | `0xDF00DF00` | 0.125 | 0.125 | **0.25** |
| 4 | 2 | 1 | `0xE700E700` | 0.25 | 0.25 | 0.25 |
| 2 | 4 | 1 | `0xE700E700` | 0.25 | 0.25 | 0.25 |
| 2 | 1 | 4 | `0xE700E700` | 0.25 | 0.25 | **0.5** |
| 16 | 8 | 4 | `0xD700D700` | 0.0625 | 0.0625 | 0.0625 |
| 8 | 16 | 4 | `0xD700D700` | 0.0625 | 0.0625 | 0.0625 |
| 8 | 4 | 16 | `0xD700D700` | 0.0625 | 0.0625 | **0.125** |

Every row is the reciprocal of the largest of the three values, wherever it
sits, and the two rows that only H1 predicts (`4,2,8` and `2,1,4`) are the
reciprocal of r2. r2 participates.

Controls in the same manifest, with 8, 4, 2 as the seeds:

| sfu | word | reading |
|:--|:--|:--|
| `rcp r0` | `0xDF00DF00` | 1/8 - the ordinary row read works |
| `rcp r1` | `0xE700E700` | 1/4 |
| `rcp r2` | `0xEF00EF00` | 1/2 |
| `rcp r4` | `0x78007800` | the pipeline value (note 53) |
| `nop r0` | `0x00000000` | no SFU result |

The same four seedings with the store in the multiplier's batch
(`mfu_r10_xyz_same.in`, `hyp-a2-20261125T073007Z`) return the identical words
(`0xDF00DF00`, `0xDF00DF00`, `0xE700E700`, `0xD700D700`), so the store position
does not change the selected value. That also settles the shape difference note
53 left open: the earlier `r10_clean` program returned `FF` because its rows were
unseeded (0) apart from a 0.5 marker, and `rcp(0.5) = 2.0` prints `FF` - the two
shapes never disagreed.

- Confirmed: the special index selects the largest of r0, r1 and r2, not the row
  the index names, and not "the larger of r0 and r1".
- Inferred: the selection is over the three coordinate components rather than
  over "all row registers", since r3 (54.4) and r5 (54.5) do not participate.

### 54.3 Sign: magnitudes, and the result is positive

A negative result cannot be read back through the store: the pure-store
calibration (`hyp-calib-20261125T072121Z`) sends every negative value to `00`
while `0.875`, `1.125`, `1.25` and `1.5` print `F5`, `F8`, `F9` and `FB`. The
sign probe therefore stores the result twice, raw in r0 and `2*result + 1` in r1,
in the ALU packet that also stores (`mfu_r10_bias.in`), so the first lane names
the magnitude and the second its sign.

`hyp-b-20261125T073011Z`:

| r0 | r1 | r2 | raw lane | bias lane | result |
|--:|--:|--:|:--|:--|:--|
| 8 | 4 | 2 | `DF` | `F9` | +0.125 |
| -8 | 4 | 2 | `DF` | `F9` | +0.125 |
| 4 | -8 | 2 | `DF` | `F9` | +0.125 |
| 4 | 2 | -8 | `DF` | `F9` | +0.125 |
| -8 | -4 | -2 | `DF` | `F9` | +0.125 |
| -2 | -8 | -4 | `DF` | `F9` | +0.125 |
| -4 | -2 | -8 | `DF` | `F9` | +0.125 |

Every negative seeding gives the same positive 0.125 that the positive seeding
gives, including the all-negative vectors and the permutations that put the
largest magnitude in r1 or r2. Two controls:

| variant | raw | bias | reading |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| `rcp r0` with r0 = -4 | `00` | `EF` | -0.25: the SFU result really is negative, and 2(-0.25)+1 = 0.5 |
| `rcp r0` with r0 = +4 | `E7` | `FB` | +0.25 and 1.5: the bias lane is the calibrated one |
| `nop r0` | `00` | `F7` | 0 and 2(0)+1 = 1.0 |

So the selector takes absolute values before the reciprocal - a signed maximum
(`-8,4,2` -> 4 -> 0.25) would have printed `E7`/`FB`, and a "largest positive,
ignoring negative magnitudes" reading (`4,-8,2` -> 4) the same. Neither happens.

- Confirmed: the magnitude is selected, the sign is dropped, and the result is
  the positive reciprocal.
- Confirmed: a negative sfu result exists and is visible in the bias lane; the
  store alone cannot show it.

### 54.4 r3 does not participate

`hyp-c-20261125T073016Z` holds r0 = 4, r1 = 2, r2 = 1 and sweeps r3:

| r3 | word | result |
|:--|:--|:--|
| 0 | `0xE700E700` | 0.25 |
| 0.25 | `0xE700E700` | 0.25 |
| 0.5 | `0xE700E700` | 0.25 |
| 2 | `0xE700E700` | 0.25 |
| 8 | `0xE700E700` | 0.25 |
| 16 | `0xE700E700` | 0.25 |
| -16 | `0xE700E700` | 0.25 |

A result of 0.25 is `1/4`, the reciprocal of r0, for every r3 including the two
values that would win a maximum (8, 16) and the negative one. The control
`rcp r0` in the same shape also returns `0xE700E700`. r3 is outside the
selection, both in magnitude and in sign.

One cross-check did not behave: the two variants that redirect the copy into r3
(`mul0: r3, sfu, #1`, store `r2,r3`) returned `0xE7000000` - the result shows up
in the r2 lane and the r3 lane reads zero - while the same redirect with
`rcp r0` as the source puts the result in r3 correctly (`dst3-r0`,
`hyp-dst-20261125T073749Z`, `0xE700F700`). That is a property of the special
source's interaction with the multipliers (54.8), not of the selection, and the
r3 sweep above does not depend on it: its result is read from two lanes that the
multipliers both write.

### 54.5 Rows above r3, and the boundaries

`hyp-e2-20261125T073356Z`, with r0 = 4, r1 = 2, r2 = 1, r3 = 0.25 and r5 seeded
from a fifth constant (`mfu_r10_row5.in`):

| r5 | word | result |
|:--|:--|:--|
| 16 | `0xE700E700` | 0.25 - r5 ignored |
| 0.0625 | `0xE700E700` | 0.25 |
| (`rcp r0` control) | `0xE700E700` | 1/4 |

`hyp-e-20261125T073349Z`, with 8, 4, 2 as the seeds:

| variant | word | result |
|:--|:--|:--|
| `rcp r8`, `r9`, `r10`, `r11`, `r12`, `r15`, `rcp r63` | `0xDF00DF00` | 0.125 each - the whole high-index group selects alike |
| `rcp r0` | `0xDF00DF00` | 1/8 |
| `rcp r4` | `0x78007800` | the pipeline value |
| 4, 4, 2 | `0xE700E700` | 0.25 - two equal maxima are fine |
| 4, 8, 8 | `0xDF00DF00` | 0.125 |
| 0, 0, 0 | `0xFF00FF00` | `rcp(0)` = +maximum, as a zero row operand does |
| 0, 4, 2 | `0xE700E700` | 0.25 |
| 0, 0, 2 | `0xEF00EF00` | 0.5 |

So every index from 8 to 15 (and 63) implements the same selection, equal maxima
tie without changing the result, a zero component is ignored while a selected
zero returns +maximum, and a row that no coordinate hypothesis includes does not
take part.

### 54.6 The row the selector picks reads back `00` when nothing copies the result

`hyp-q2-20261125T073406Z` runs the special index with both multipliers removed,
so the store reads the seeded rows themselves.

| seeds | store pair | word | reading |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| 8, 4, 2, 0.25 | `r0,r1` | `0xFF000000` | r0 (the maximum) reads 0, r1 reads 4 |
| 4, 8, 2, 0.25 | `r0,r1` | `0x0000FF00` | r0 reads 4, r1 (the maximum) reads 0 |
| 4, 2, 8, 0.25 | `r0,r1` | `0xFF00FF00` | both read their seeds (the maximum was r2) |
| 4, 2, 0.5, 0.25 | `r0,r1` | `0xFF000000` | r0 (the maximum) reads 0 |
| 8, 4, 2, 0.25, `nop r0` | `r0,r1` | `0xFF00FF00` | without the sfu everything reads |

For the r0/r1 pair this is consistent in every run: the row that held the maximum
reads `00` afterwards. The r2/r3 pair does not follow the same rule - `8,4,2`
reads r2 as `00` although the maximum is r0, `4,2,8` reads r2 as `FF`, and
`4,2,0.5` reads r2 as `00` - so the r2 lane of that pair is not reliable in this
shape.

- Confirmed: with no multiplier copying it, the selected row of the r0/r1 pair
  reads back as zero after the special sfu runs, and without the sfu it does
  not.
- Unknown: whether that is a destructive read, a write of a value whose high byte
  is zero, or a store-path effect, and why the r2/r3 pair disagrees. Every
  measurement in 54.2 to 54.5 avoids the question by copying the SFU result into
  both stored lanes. **Addressed by note 55.6:** the rows really are disturbed
  (the store, an ALU copy and a later MFU word all agree), the r2 lane of the
  r2/r3 pair is reading that disturbed row 2, and the value's encoding is still
  unresolved.

### 54.7 The magnitude relationship is a reciprocal

`hyp-d-20261125T073344Z`, store pair `r2,r3` so the r2 lane names a coordinate
and the r3 lane the result (the reciprocal is therefore in byte 3):

| r0 | r1 | r2 | word | result |
|--:|--:|--:|:--|:--|
| 2 | 1 | 0.5 | `0xEF00EF00` | 0.5 |
| 4 | 2 | 1 | `0xE700E700` | 0.25 |
| 8 | 4 | 2 | `0xDF00DF00` | 0.125 |
| 1 | 0.5 | 2 | `0xEF00EF00` | 0.5 |
| 0.5 | 2 | 1 | `0xEF00EF00` | 0.5 |
| 6 | 1 | 0.5 | `0xE100E100` | 1/6 |
| 3 | 3 | 0.5 | `0xE900E900` | 1/3 |
| 0.5 | 6 | 1 | `0xE100E100` | 1/6 |

The powers of two land on their exact codes and the unequal ratios select the
same component wherever it sits. The two non-power-of-two cases read `E1` and
`E9`; the same program with `rcp r0` of a 6.0 and a 3.0 row returns `E1` and
`E9` (`hyp-mx-20261125T073545Z`, `acc-rcp-6`, `acc-rcp-3`), so the selector
computes the same reciprocal those ordinary reads compute, one readback code
below the true 1/6 and 1/3 (a code is 9% on this conversion).

- Confirmed: the output is the reciprocal of the selected magnitude, not merely a
  monotonic function of it.

### 54.8 The compiler's word: the multipliers

The row-based multiplies are exact whenever the word does not name an index at or
above 8 as the sfu source. With seeds r0 = 1.5, r1 = 3.0, r2 = 0.5 the two
multiplies `mul0: r0, r2, r0` and `mul1: r1, r2, r1` should produce 0.75 (`F3`)
and 1.5 (`FB`):

| sfu source | word | r0 lane | r1 lane |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| none (`nop r0`) | `0xFB00F300` | 0.75 | 1.5 |
| `rcp r4` | `0xFB00F300` | 0.75 | 1.5 |
| `rcp r3` | `0xFB00F300` | 0.75 | 1.5 |
| `rcp r8` | `0xF6000000` | 0.92 | 0 |
| `rcp r9` | `0xFF00F600` | >=2.0 | 0.92 |
| `rcp r10` | `0xEE000000` | 0.5 | 0 |
| `rcp r15` | `0xFF000000` | >=2.0 | 0 |

`hyp-mul-20261125T073400Z` again reproduces note 53's `F1000000` for its own
seeding (`both-r10`), and `mul0-only-r10` reproduces it too while
`mul1-only-r10` returns `0xFB000000`: only the lane the surviving multiply writes
changes.

Feeding the special result to the multipliers in the separate-store shape
(`hyp-mx-20261125T073545Z`) shows the units do receive it - `mul0: r0, sfu, #1`
and `mul1: r1, sfu, #1` give `0xE900E900`, the 1/3 code - but a row-times-row
multiply in the same word does not compute its operands:

| word | word as stored | r0 lane | r1 lane | expected |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| `mul0: r0, r2, r0` both muls | `0xEE000000` | 0.5 | 0 | 0.75, 1.5 |
| `mul0: r0, r2, #1` both muls | `0xE900E900` | 1/3 | 1/3 | 0.5, 0.5 |
| `mul0: r0, r2, r1` both muls | `0x00000000` | 0 | 0 | 1.5, 1.5 |
| `mul0: r0, sfu, #1` only | `0xE9000000` | 1/3 | - | - |
| `rcp r3` + both row muls | `0xFB00F300` | 0.75 | 1.5 | 0.75, 1.5 |
| `rcp r3` + `mul0` only | `0xFF00F300` | 0.75 | 3.0 | 0.75 |

The 1/3 code appears where a multiply by `#1` should have produced 0.5, and the
0.5 that a `r2 * r0` should have produced also appears, so the perturbation is not
"the multipliers are dead" or "the muls always pass the SFU result"; it is some
interaction between the special source, the row operands and the destination
that this note does not identify.

- Confirmed: the same two multiplies are exact with `nop`, `rcp r3` or `rcp r4`
  as the sfu source and are not exact with `rcp r8`, `r9`, `r10` or `r15`.
- Confirmed: the special SFU result itself is readable by a multiply
  (`mul0: ..., sfu, #1` returns the 1/3 code) and the perturbation is confined to
  words that use a high index as the sfu source.
- Unknown: the rule. The smallest next experiment is a sweep of one multiply's
  `src0`, `src1` and `dst` fields over rows 0..3, `sfu` and `#1` with
  `sfu: rcp r10` fixed, storing the pair the destination lands in.

**Resolved by note 55.** That sweep was run: the multipliers are ordinary, a
source field naming row 2 delivers `1/M`, and the row holding the maximum of
r0..r2 delivers 0. The compiler's word therefore computes `r0/M` and `r1/M`
with the major axis zeroed, which re-decodes the `0xF1000000` above. See note
55.3 to 55.5.

### 54.9 What this means for the cube path

`1 / max(|x|, |y|, |z|)` is exactly the major-axis reciprocal a cube-map
projection needs: the face coordinates are `(x/M, y/M)`, and the blob's uniform
coordinate path scales the two components by that factor instead of dividing.
The measurements here confirm the selector's arithmetic and therefore the
intended meaning of `sfu: rcp r10` in the compiler's word: it names the
major-axis reciprocal, not a row.

What remains unexplained is the other half of that word. Its two multiplies read
`r2` and the rows, not the SFU result, and on this hardware they do not compute
their operands while the special source is in the word (54.8). So the word is
consistent with "compute 1/M, then scale x and y by it" only if the multiply's
`r2` operand is what the special mode substitutes, which the measurements neither
confirm nor exclude.

- Confirmed: `rcp r10` selects the largest absolute component of r0, r1 and r2
  and returns its reciprocal, with the sign dropped and r3 and r5 excluded.
- Confirmed: with a high index as the sfu source the row-based multiplies in the
  same word are not exact; with a row source or no sfu they are.
- Unknown: the multiply rule, and whether the selector reads the same rows when
  an `ipl` fills them instead of the ALU (the harness writes them through the
  ALU here). The smallest experiment for the latter is one `ipl` of a varying
  into r0..r2 with the same probe shape.
- Not required for GLES2: which TEX field selects a cube face; the harness still
  has no cube binding path (note 51).

### 54.10 Runs and method notes

`hyp-calib-20261125T072121Z` (12 variants, 40 runs),
`hyp-pair-20261125T072446Z` (14, 46),
`hyp-q-20261125T072706Z` (9, 31),
`hyp-lane-20261125T073209Z` (4, 16),
`hyp-a-20261125T072948Z` (14, 46),
`hyp-a2-20261125T073007Z` (4, 16),
`hyp-b-20261125T073011Z` (10, 34),
`hyp-c-20261125T073016Z` (10, 34),
`hyp-d-20261125T073344Z` (8, 28),
`hyp-e-20261125T073349Z` (14, 46),
`hyp-e2-20261125T073356Z` (3, 13),
`hyp-mul-20261125T073400Z` (12, 40),
`hyp-q2-20261125T073406Z` (9, 31),
`hyp-mx-20261125T073545Z` (15, 49),
`hyp-dst-20261125T073749Z` (6, 22). Every manifest carries the two standard
controls; no run in these fifteen failed or timed out.

Two runs are not evidence: `hyp-a-20261125T072149Z` was rejected by the
assembler (42 of 46 runs failed to parse because its store named the pair
`r3,r3`, which the DW does not accept), and `hyp-a-20261125T072306Z` used a
single-multiply capture that read a seeded lane back and showed the disturbance
of 54.6; the two-multiplier capture replaced it.

Method notes:

- `store rt1, r2, r3` reads `[r3.hi][r3.lo][r2.hi][r2.lo]`: byte 1 is the first
  operand and byte 3 the second, in both the same-batch and later-batch shapes
  (`hyp-lane-20261125T073209Z`: `0.125, 0.5` in r0/r1 reads `0xEF00DF00` and
  `4.0, 0.25` in r2/r3 reads `0xE700FF00`). An earlier template here stored two
  rows and read its lanes in the other order; that reading was wrong and is not
  used.
- The store conversion maps negative values to `00`; `2*result + 1` in a second
  lane is what makes a sign visible, and the bias lane is calibrated
  (`0.875` `F5`, `1.125` `F8`, `1.25` `F9`, `1.5` `FB`).
- `sfu: nop` needs its row operand (`nop r0`), and an empty multiply line is
  legal inside the MFU block; a bare `mul1: NOP` is not.
- The readback is quantised to 8 codes per octave, so a non-power-of-two
  reciprocal is only compared against the code an ordinary `rcp` of the same
  value produces, never against the real number.

## 55. The cube-coordinate word: the multipliers work, row 2 carries 1/M, and the major axis reads zero

Note 54 left the compiler's cube word half explained: `sfu: rcp r10` is the
maximum-absolute-component reciprocal, but the two multipliers in the same word
did not compute their own operands. This note separates the multiplier from the
store, sweeps its source fields, destinations and slots, and finds that the
multipliers are ordinary - the word's `r2` operand is the reciprocal, not row 2 -
but that the row holding the selected maximum delivers zero to the multiplier.
It then feeds the selector from the interpolator instead of the ALU and finds the
same arithmetic, with the fetch of a bundle invisible to the sfu in that bundle.
The cube-face question is still unmeasured, and the reasons are hardware and
tooling here, not silence: the blob compiler is absent from this device and
libgrate has no cube-map binding path.

Measured on the Toshiba AC100 (`7.2.0-gd1efa725955b`, Tegra 20, AR20 fragment
path), branch `code/fragment-isa-experiments` at `6ad1e92` (fetched from
`i-am-cipher-null/grate` at measurement time, identical to the local head).
Three repeats per variant, controls before and after every manifest
(`immediate-operand` `0x000000FF`, `varying-pipeline` `0xFF01FF00`). Sixteen
manifests, 815 runs, **no failed, timed-out or rejected run**. Raw words,
generated `.asm`, stdout and stderr are in the run directories listed in 55.10.

### 55.1 Verdicts

- **Priority 1 (multipliers with `rcp r10`): resolved for the source, destination
  and slot fields.** A multiplier source field that names row 2 delivers the
  special reciprocal `1/M` instead of the row's value; the fields `sfu` and `#1`
  deliver `1/M` and `1.0`; rows 0, 1 and 3 deliver their contents; the order of
  the two fields, the destination row, and the multiplier slot do not change any
  of this. Note 53's and note 54's "the multipliers do not compute their
  operands" is **disproved**: they do, with a substituted operand. *(Refined in
  56.5: the substitution is specific to index 10; 8..15 are otherwise equivalent
  for the reciprocal itself, and under every other member of the family the `r0`
  and `r2` fields carry each other's row.)*
- **Priority 1 (selected-row side effect): confirmed as a register effect, with
  an unresolved encoding.** After a special-index word the rows holding the
  maximum read back as values that are not their inputs, in the DW store, in an
  ALU copy, and still after another MFU word, while shadow copies of the same
  seeds in rows 4..7 stay intact. What the rows contain is *not* determined
  here. *(Resolved in 56.1, 56.3 and 56.7: bits 5:4 of the operand field are
  ignored by the decode, low nibble 8..15 selects the normalization family, and
  rows 0..2 receive a descriptor plus a negated operand by a measured rule that
  holds across 30 vectors.)*
- **Priority 2 (IPL): confirmed.** IPL operand slot *k* fetches component *k* of
  the varying into row *k*, and the selector computes the same
  `1/max(|x|,|y|,|z|)` over interpolated rows as over ALU-written ones, including
  the sign drop and the `r2`-operand substitution. An IPL fetch in the same MFU
  word as the sfu is **not** visible to that word's sfu; a fetch in an earlier
  word is.
- **Priority 3 (where face selection happens): unresolved, and not measurable in
  this tree.** The blob compiler is unavailable (`CgDrv_Create: BLOB compiler is
  unavailable`, no `libcgdrv.so` on the device), so the `textureCube` variants
  cannot be compiled, and libgrate has no cube-map binding, so no cube sample can
  be taken. The one available compiler word is decoded in 55.8 and its arithmetic
  matches Mesa's `nir_normalize_cubemap_coords` lowering, but the TEX word of
  that dump is not in this checkout and no face selection was observed.

### 55.2 The probe shape

`tests/grate/asm/mfu_p1.in`: batch 0 seeds r0..r3 from fragment constants
(`.lh`, fp20), batch 1 is the MFU word under test and stores nothing, batch 2
copies one row into r0/r1 through the ALU, batch 3 stores the r0/r1 pair. Keeping
the store out of the MFU batch removes the store-timing confound, and always
storing r0/r1 means no result depends on the r2/r3 pair, whose r2 lane turned out
to be unreliable (55.6).

Readback is the calibrated store byte of note 53: `store rt1, X, Y` writes
`code(X)` in byte 1 and `code(Y)` in byte 3, `code = 0xF7 + 8*log2(value)`, `0.0`
is `00`, and 2.0 or more clips to `FF`.

Two calibration results make the rest readable:

| variant | word | reading |
|:--|:--|:--|
| `calib-copy-lo` (`MAD r0.lh, r2, #1, #0`, r2 = 0.5) | `0xEF00EF00` | a bare row source preserves the fp20 code |
| `calib-copy-h` (`r2.h`) | `0xF600F600` | the `.h` half does **not** hold the value |
| `calib-mfu-copy` (`rcp r3` then `mul0: r3, sfu, #1`, r3 = 1.0) | `0xF700F700` | MFU to ALU to store is exact |

The seed patterns keep the selected maximum out of the operands wherever the
question allows it: A = 4.0, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0 (maximum in r0), B = 0.25, 4.0, 0.5,
1.0 (in r1), C = 0.25, 0.5, 4.0, 1.0 (in r2), so `1/M = 0.25` in every pattern and
the ordinary product `r2 * #1` is a clean `0.5`.

### 55.3 The source sweep

`p1_sweep0`/`p1_sweep1`, 36 ordered source pairs in each multiplier slot,
destination r3 (byte 3) or r2 (byte 1) when the pair itself contains r3. The grid
is the measured result byte; every cell was checked against the model in
`check_p1.py` and all 72 cells match (0 mismatches, both slots):

| src0 \ src1 | r0 | r1 | r2 | r3 | sfu | #1 |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| **r0** | `D7` | `DF` | `D7` | `E7` | `D7` | `E7` |
| **r1** | `DF` | `D7` | `D7` | `E7` | `D7` | `E7` |
| **r2** | `D7` | `D7` | `D7` | `E7` | `D7` | `E7` |
| **r3** | `E7` | `E7` | `E7` | `F7` | `E7` | `F7` |
| **sfu** | `D7` | `D7` | `D7` | `E7` | `D7` | `E7` |
| **#1** | `E7` | `E7` | `E7` | `F7` | `E7` | `F7` |

With `1/M = 0.25` (`E7`), 0.0625 = `D7`, 0.125 = `DF` and 1.0 = `F7`, the grid
reads as: any field naming row 2 gives `1/M`, the field `sfu` gives `1/M`, `#1`
gives 1.0, row 3 gives 1.0, and rows 0/1 give the pattern's value for that row.
The multiplier slot does not matter: the `mul1` grid is byte-identical to the
`mul0` grid in all 36 cells.

### 55.4 Destinations

`hyp-p1dst`, 54 variants: six diagnostic pairs in each destination row r0..r3 and
each slot, with pattern C (maximum in r2), plus six pattern-A cases where r0 or r2
holds the maximum. Store pair r0/r1 for destinations r0/r1 and r2/r3 for r2/r3.

| case | expected | r0 | r1 | r2 | r3 | mul1 |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| `sfu * #1` | `E7` | `E7` | `E7` | `E7` | `E7` | same |
| `#1 * sfu` | `E7` | `E7` | `E7` | `E7` | `E7` | same |
| `r0 * r1` | `DF` | `DF` | `DF` | `DF` | `DF` | same |
| `r2 * r0` | `D7` | `D7` | `D7` | `D7` | `D7` | same |
| `r2 * r1` | `DF` | `DF` | `DF` | `DF` | `DF` | same |
| `r3 * #1` | `F7` | `F7` | `F7` | `F7` | `F7` | same |

Every cell matched its prediction in all three repeats. Writing a row that holds
the maximum is not special (`da-r0-r0-c1` `0xE7000000`: the result lane is `00`
because the *operand* r0 was cleared, the untouched lane keeps `E7`), and the
slot-symmetric cells are byte-identical.

### 55.5 The compiler's word, and what it computes

`hyp-p1word` and `hyp-p1rows`, store r0/r1 so byte 1 is r0 and byte 3 is r1:

| variant | word | r0 lane | r1 lane | reading |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| `w-both` = `rcp r10`, `r0 = r2*r0`, `r1 = r2*r1` | `0xD7000000` | `00` | `D7` | 0.25 x 0 = 0, 0.25 x 0.25 = 0.0625 |
| `w-both-c` (same, maximum in r2) | `0xDF00D700` | `D7` | `DF` | 0.25 x 0.25, 0.25 x 0.5 |
| `w-both-f` (maximum in r0, r2 = 0.25) | `0xDF000000` | `00` | `DF` | 0.25 x 0, 0.25 x 0.5 |
| `w-mul0only` | `0xE7000000` | `00` | `E7` | the untouched r1 lane keeps 0.25 |
| `w-mul1only` | `0xD7000000` | `00` | `D7` | r0 reads 0 although nothing wrote it |
| `w-swapsrc` (`r0*r2`, `r1*r2`) | `0xD7000000` | `00` | `D7` | operand order is irrelevant |
| `w-swapdst` | `0x0000D700` | `D7` | `00` | destinations follow the field |
| `w-sfusrc` (`sfu*r0`, `sfu*r1`) | `0xD7000000` | `00` | `D7` | an explicit `sfu` equals the `r2` field |
| `w-const` (``#1`*r0`, `#1*r1`) | `0xE7000000` | `00` | `E7` | the clear is independent of the sources |
| `w-both-nosfu` | `0xDF00FF00` | `FF` | `DF` | 0.5 x 4 = 2.0 and 0.5 x 0.25 |
| `w-both-r4` (`rcp r4`) | `0xDF00FF00` | `FF` | `DF` | an ordinary source keeps the products |

So the compiler's word computes `r0 = r0/M` and `r1 = r1/M` where `M = max(|r0|,
|r1|, |r2|)`, with one exception: the lane whose coordinate *is* the maximum comes
out zero. This also re-decodes note 53's `0xF1000000` (seeds 1.5, 1.0, 0.5: r0 =
max, `1/M = 0.667`, so r0 = 0 and r1 = 0.667 = `F1`) and note 54's `0xEE000000`
(seeds 1.5, 3.0, 0.5: r1 = max, so r0 = 0.5 = `EE` and r1 = 0). Both were
previously read as evidence that the multipliers were broken; they are not.

- Confirmed: the multiplier source naming row 2 delivers `1/M`; the other rows,
  `sfu` and `#1` deliver their ordinary values; order, destination and slot are
  irrelevant. *(Refined in 56.5: `r2` carries `1/M` only under index 10; under 8,
  9 and 11..15 it carries row 0's pre-word value, and the `r0` field carries row
  2's.)*
- Confirmed: the compiler's word computes the two projected coordinates with the
  major axis zeroed, on this hardware and in these shapes.
- Disproved: notes 53 and 54's reading that the multipliers stop computing their
  operands in a special-index word.

### 55.6 The row that held the maximum

Rows 0..2 do not keep their values across a special-index word. The evidence, all
three repeats identical:

| probe | word | reading |
|:--|:--|:--|
| `rd-r0-a`: maximum in r0, no multipliers, read r0 | `0x00000000` | r0 = 0 in the store lane **and** in the ALU copy |
| `rd-r1-b`: maximum in r1, read r1 | `0x00000000` | the same for r1 |
| `rd-r0-b`, `rd-r1-a`: those rows not the maximum | `0xE700E700` | 0.25, untouched |
| `rd-r3-a`: read r3 | `0xF700F700` | 1.0, untouched |
| `rd-r2-a` (maximum in r0) | `0x00000000` | r2 = 0 |
| `rd-r2-b` (maximum in r1) | `0xFF00FF00` | r2 >= 2.0 |
| `r2-c/d/e/g/h` (maximum in r2, all equal, 2.0 maximum) | `0xFF00FF00` | r2 >= 2.0 |
| `r2-f` (maximum in r0, r2 = 0.25) | `0x00000000` | r2 = 0 |
| `dl-r0-a` (row read after another MFU word) | `0x00000000` | the disturbance persists |
| `dl-sh01-a` (shadow rows 4 and 5 = seeds 4.0 and 0.25) | `0xE700FF00` | the seeds themselves are intact |
| `dl-r0-r3` (same shape, `rcp r3`) | `0xFF00FF00` | without a special index r0 keeps 4.0 |

`r2`'s content is not the reciprocal, not its seed, and depends on which row held
the maximum (`00` when that is r0, `FF` otherwise) and, with interpolated inputs,
on the sign: `n-negx8-read-r0` reads `0.5` (`0xEF00EF00`) for x = -8 where
`n-pos8-…` reads `00`. The multiplier operand for that row follows the same sign
split: `n-pos-mul-r0` and `n-pos8-mul-r0` give `00`, `n-negx-mul-r0` and
`n-negx8-mul-r0` give `0.5`, independent of the magnitude (4 and 8 both `0.5`).

One probe disagrees with the others and is reported rather than smoothed: reading
r0 through `abs()` after a special word returns `0.5` (`n-pos8-abs-r0`
`0xFF00EF00`) where the plain copy of the same row returns `0`
(`n-pos8-mul-r0`). `abs()` itself is faithful on an ordinary row (`abs-plain`:
`abs(0.5)` reads `0xE700EF00`), so the discrepancy is in the special-index read
path, not in the ALU instruction. *(Resolved in 56.8: the row holds a negative
value, -0.5 in this case. The plain copy and the store code cannot show a
negative number, `abs()` and a negated read show the magnitude, and all eleven
calibrated read forms agree once the sign is accounted for. It is not a read
path anomaly.)*

- Confirmed: the disturbance is a register-level effect, not a store artifact -
  two independent read paths agree in the plain cases, it survives a following MFU
  word, and shadow rows are untouched.
- Confirmed: the row holding a *positive* maximum delivers 0 to a multiplier, so
  the compiler's major-axis lane is 0. *(Refined in 56.7 and 56.9: the row holds
  the negated operand, so that lane is `-sign(x) * 1/M`. It reads `00` because the
  store code hides the sign; the compiler word's `r0` lane is genuinely negative
  (-0.125 for x = 4, M = 4), not zero.)*
- Unresolved *(answered in 56.7 and 56.8 for the family 8..15)*: what the
  disturbed rows contain. The `abs()` contradiction and the sign dependence of
  the value are not explained by "the row is cleared". 56.7 gives the rule (row 2
  <- `2d + (s < 0)`, one coordinate row <- `-s * its pre-word value`) and 56.8
  shows the sign that `abs()` exposed.
- Operationally: a shader must not read rows 0..2 after a word with a
  special-index sfu source; the reciprocal is only reachable through the
  multiplier's `r2` or `sfu` field. *(Refined in 56.10: `r2` only under index 10;
  `sfu` under all of 8..15.)*

### 55.7 Interpolated inputs

`tests/grate/asm/mfu_p2.in` fetches with one MFU word and runs the sfu in the
next; `vs_tram_c.in` exports four constants as the varying and
`tram_linker.in`/`LINK fp20, fp20, fp20, fp20, tram0.xyzw, export1` binds them to
TRAM0.

| variant | word | reading |
|:--|:--|:--|
| `m-r0` (x, y, z = 1.0, 0.5, 0.25) | `0xEF00F700` | r0 = 1.0, r1 = 0.5 |
| `m-r1` | `0x0000E700` | r2 = 0.25, r3 unfed = 0 |
| `m-rcp-r0` / `m-rcp-r1` / `m-rcp-r2` | `0xF700E700` / `0xFF00E700` / `0xFF00FF00` | 1/x = 1.0, 1/y = 2.0, 1/z = 4.0 |
| `ctl-unfed-rows` (no fetch) | `0x00000000` | rows are zero, so nothing is stale |

IPL operand slot *k* writes row *k* with varying component *k*: `ipl: t0.fp20,
t0.fp20, t0.fp20, NOP` puts x, y and z in r0, r1 and r2.

The selector over interpolated coordinates, `1/M = 0.25` in every case:

| variant | coordinates | word | reading |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| `sel-maxx` | 4.0, 2.0, 0.5 | `0xE7000000` | `E7` |
| `sel-maxy` | 2.0, 4.0, 0.5 | `0xE700FF00` | `E7` |
| `sel-maxz` | 2.0, 0.5, 4.0 | `0xE700FF00` | `E7` |
| `sel-negx` | -4.0, 2.0, 0.5 | `0xE700F700` | `E7` - sign dropped |
| `n-negy` / `n-negz` | -4.0 in y / z | `0xE700FF00` | `E7` |
| `n-negx-mag` | -2.0, 1.0, 0.5 | `0xEF00F700` | `EF` - 1/2 |
| `sel-mul-r2` | 4.0, 2.0, 0.5, `r2 * #1` | `0xE7000000` | `0.25`, not the fetched 0.5: row 2 is substituted here too |
| `sel-read-r0` | 4.0, 2.0, 0.5, read r0 | `0x00000000` | the maximum row is cleared with interpolated inputs as well |

Same-bundle ordering, with the rows pre-seeded by the ALU to 1.0, 2.0, 0.5, 1.0
(`1/M = 0.5`) and the fetch carrying 4.0, 2.0, 0.5 (`1/M = 0.25`):

| variant | word | reading |
|:--|:--|:--|
| `b-same-sel` (fetch and `rcp r10` in one word) | `0xEF00EF00` | `0.5` - the sfu used the **pre-fetch** rows |
| `b-same-mul-r2` | `0xEF00EF00` | the substituted operand is the same pre-fetch value |
| `b-same-read-r0` / `b-same-read-r1` | `0xFF00FF00` | after the word the rows hold the fetched 4.0 and 2.0 |
| `b-fetch-nosfu` | `0xF700EF00` | the fetch did write r2 = 0.5 |
| `b-prev-sel` (no fetch at all) | `0xEF00FF00` | `0.5` - the pre-word seeds |
| `sel-same` in the p2 shape (nothing seeded, fetch + `rcp r10`) | `0xFF00EF00` | `rcp(0)` = `FF` while r2 shows the fetched 0.5 |
| `sel-same-mul-r2` | `0xFF00EF00` | the substituted value is `rcp` of the *old* rows |

- Confirmed: the selector reads interpolated rows and returns the same
  major-axis reciprocal, with the sign dropped and row 2 substituted.
- Confirmed: an IPL fetch in the same MFU word is not visible to that word's sfu;
  the fetch's writes are visible to following words.
- Inferred: the sfu reads its operand set at the start of the word, before the
  fetch writes rows.

### 55.8 The compiler word, decoded, and the cube path

`0x28689402 0x00000000` is the whole MFU word the proprietary compiler emits for a
uniform `vec3` samplerCube coordinate. The two halves are `[muls, opcode, reg]`
then `[var0..var3]`, and decoding the bits with `fragment_asm.h` gives:

| field | bits | value |
|:--|:--|:--|
| `mul0_src0`, `mul0_src1`, `mul0_dst` | 0..3, 4..7, 8..10 | 2, 0, 4 = row 2 x row 0 into row 0 |
| `mul1_src0`, `mul1_src1`, `mul1_dst` | 11..14, 15..18, 19..21 | 2, 1, 5 = row 2 x row 1 into row 1 |
| `opcode`, `reg` | 22..25, 26..31 | 1, 10 = `rcp r10` |
| `var0..var3` | other half | 0, so `ipl: NOP, NOP, NOP, NOP` |

So the word is exactly `sfu: rcp r10`, `mul0: r0, r2, r0`, `mul1: r1, r2, r1`,
with no varying fetch - which fits a *uniform* coordinate (the varying version of
the same operation appears in `tests/grate/asm/cube2_fs.txt` as `sfu: rcp r4` with
`ipl: t0.fp20, t0.fp20, NOP, NOP`, the perspective-correction word, not a
major-axis one).

The arithmetic matches Mesa's NIR lowering for cube sampling
(`src/compiler/nir/nir_normalize_cubemap_coords.c`), which computes
`norm = fmax_abs_vec_comp(xyz)` and `normalized = coord * frcp(norm)`, leaving
face selection to the texture unit. zink takes the other route
(`zink_lower_cubemap_to_array.c`, rewriting the sampler to a 2D array), which
would put the face index in the shader. AR20's word matches the first: scale by
the reciprocal of the major axis and hand the result to TEX.

What cannot be settled here:

- **No compiler.** `~/build/grate-exp/tools/cgc` prints `CgDrv_Create: BLOB
  compiler is unavailable`; `src/libcgc/dummy.c` is the fallback and no
  `libcgdrv.so` exists on the device (`find / -maxdepth 4 -name 'libcgdrv*'` is
  empty). `textureCube(dir)`, `textureCube(dir * 2.0)`, `textureCube(normalize(dir))`
  and the colour-output variant cannot be compiled, so their words cannot be
  compared. The single word above comes from an earlier dump and is not
  reproducible on this device.
- **No cube binding.** `GL_TEXTURE_CUBE_MAP` appears nowhere in `src/`, `include/`
  or `tests/`; `TEXTURE_DESC1` carries only filters, format, compression and the
  two wrap modes, and `TEXTURE_DESC2` only width, height, the log2 encodings, LOD
  and mipmap flags, with no dimension or face field. A cube sample would first
  need a texture-target path in libgrate, which is the subsystem the brief says
  not to build here.
- **No TEX word for the cube shader.** The dump this word came from is not in the
  checkout, so the TEX instruction, coordinate registers and sampler bits of the
  real cube shader are unknown.

Nothing above shows that face selection is fixed-function. It shows that the
shader-side half of the operation (the major-axis reciprocal and the two scaled
coordinates) matches a normalizing lowering, and that the remaining half is
unmeasured.

### 55.9 Open questions and the smallest experiments

1. What do the disturbed rows contain, and why does `abs()` disagree with the
   plain copy in the positive case? Next: repeat `rd-r0-a` with the read two and
   three MFU words later, and with the value read through a negation and through
   a multiply by `#1` in the same run, to separate "the row is written" from "the
   read port returns another register". *(Answered in 56.8: the row is written,
   the read port is faithful, the value is negative. The delay half of the
   experiment is `s56_delay`.)*
2. Is the major-axis zero also what the blob's own shader produces, or is it a
   property of this harness's row seeding? Next: the same word with the
   coordinates arriving from IPL *and* the maximum in each component, read from a
   second row that a multiplier copies (partly done here: `sel-read-r0` reads 0
   for interpolated input too, so the ALU is not the cause). *(Partly answered in
   56.9: with ALU-seeded rows the compiler word's major-axis lane is `-0.125`,
   i.e. `1/M` times the negated operand, so the zero is the store code's sign
   loss. The blob's own words remain unmeasured.)*
3. Which TEX fields carry the face? Next: a cube-map binding path in libgrate (a
   real subsystem, out of scope here), then the descriptor bit sweep of note 51
   repeated with a cube sampler bound.
4. Which words does the compiler emit for the four `textureCube` variants? Next:
   run `tools/cgc` where `libcgdrv.so` is available and diff the two halves.

### 55.10 Runs and method notes

Evidence: `p1-pilot-20261125T171546Z` (30 variants, 94 runs),
`p1-rows-20261125T171852Z` (17, 55), `p1-rows2-20261125T172049Z` (14, 46),
`p1-word-20261125T172131Z` (12, 40), `p1-sweep0-20261125T172251Z` (36, 112),
`p1-sweep1-20261125T172304Z` (36, 112), `p1-dst-20261125T172453Z` (54, 166),
`p1-delay-20261125T172605Z` (15, 49), `p1-rows-20261125T173419Z` (1, 7, the
`abs-plain` calibration), `p2-ipl-20261125T173338Z` (36, 112),
`p2-same-bundle-20261125T173042Z` (6, 22). Every manifest keeps the two standard
controls; all runs pass.

Superseded, not evidence: `p2-ipl-20261125T172814Z`, `…173030Z`, `…173156Z`,
`…173247Z` (the negative-varying variants were progressively added, and the first
set had `ipl: NOP` with a single MFU word, so it never fetched; 0 failures but no
usable negative data), and `p2-same-bundle-20261125T172938Z` (its seeds happened
to equal the fetched values, so the same-bundle ordering was not discriminated).

Method notes:

- Directions: `store rt1, X, Y` is byte 1 = X and byte 3 = Y; the byte 3 code is
  the two leading hex digits of the printed word. Three readings in this note were
  corrected after reading the wrong byte first, and the note 54 lane calibration
  was re-checked against it.
- A bare row source (`MAD r0.lh, r2, #1, #0`) preserves the fp20 code; `r2.h` does
  not, so every row copy here uses a bare source.
- An MFU block may hold `sfu:` and `ipl:` alone; an empty multiplier line leaves
  the line blank, which is legal, while `mul1: NOP` is a parse error.
- The ALU's `abs()` form (`abs(r0)`) is accepted by the ALU grammar and is faithful
  on ordinary rows.
- The two probe shapes differ only in where the store sits (55.2), and the same
  operands return the same codes in both, so no read-after-write correction is
  applied anywhere.

## 56. The sfu operand field: 8..15 are one normalization family, index 10 is the one a multiplier can use, and the row writes are a descriptor plus a negated operand

Runs on the AC100 (Toshiba AC100, Tegra 20 / AR20, `armv7l`, kernel
`7.2.0-gd1efa725955b`) at experiment-branch head
`a7775c453522f6f6de201199c2cc463d2e76b4d3`, which is also
`github/code/fragment-isa-experiments` at the time of the first manifest. Note 55
is the baseline throughout; this note only adds to it.

### 56.1 Verdicts

- **Confirmed:** the six-bit `reg` operand field is decoded with bits 5:4
  ignored. Every one of the 16 low-nibble values gives the *same 32-bit store
  word* at `r`, `r+16`, `r+32` and `r+48`, for all three axis permutations
  (48 groups, 0 mismatches). This is a hardware decode property, not an
  assembler truncation: Grate's own disassembler round-trips the generated word
  as `sfu: rcp r50` when the source says `rcp r50` (56.11).
- **Confirmed:** low nibble `0..3` reads rows 0..3, and `rcp` **writes the
  reciprocal back into the row it read** (row 0 = 0.25 after `rcp r0` with
  r0 = 4.0; the write is visible to a later ALU read, a later word's multiplier
  and a later word's sfu).
- **Confirmed:** low nibble `8..15` returns `1/M`, `M = max(|r0|,|r1|,|r2|)`, for
  all eight values, and **does not read a row at all**: with every row set to 1.0
  and the matching row alone set to 0.5 or 4.0 (`s56_idx`), all eight return the
  same code for both.
- **Confirmed:** low nibble `4..7` does not read the seeded rows either, and its
  result does not change with row content (`idx-4`: `0x78` for both tests;
  `idx-5..7`: `00` for both).
- **Confirmed:** for opcodes other than `rcp`, the index is decoded as
  `reg & 7`: `rsq r10` equals `rsq r2` byte-for-byte, `rsq r12` equals
  `rsq r4`, and so on (56.6). Bit 3 changes the behaviour of `rcp` only.
- **Confirmed:** inside the same MFU word, the multiplier source `r2` delivers
  `1/M` for index **10 only**; for 8, 9 and 11..15 it delivers row 0's pre-word
  value. The source `sfu` delivers `1/M` for all eight.
- **Confirmed:** after a normalization-family `rcp`, the ALU-visible rows 0..2
  hold a *descriptor* and a *negated operand* (the rule is in 56.7); rows 3..15
  keep their values, and the written rows stay unchanged across up to two later
  MFU words and across later ALU batch boundaries. *(Refined by 57.6: the
  descriptor/operand pair is written by any single-step sfu instruction whose
  operand field has bit 3 set, not only by `rcp`.)*
- **Disproved:** "the special selector is 10". For `rcp`, 8..15 are equivalent
  in the reciprocal and in the row writes; 10 is distinguished only by what a
  multiplier reads from row 2. Note 55's statement that a multiplier source
  naming row 2 supplies `1/M` is true for the compiler's word, which uses 10,
  but is not a property of the family.
- **Disproved:** the note 55 report that reading through `abs()` disagrees with a
  plain read. The row holds a **negative** value (r0 = -0.5 in the positive-x
  case); the plain read and the store code cannot show a negative number, while
  `abs()` shows 0.5 and a negating read shows +0.5. Every read path agrees once
  the sign is accounted for (56.8).
- **Unresolved:** what low nibble 4..7 actually reads (a large positive value for
  4, negative or zero for 5..7, independent of the row table); why bits 5:4 are
  ignored by the hardware; why the multiplier operand fetch sees the written row
  0 but the pre-word row 2; and the mechanism that produces the negated operand
  and the axis/sign code (the rule is reproducible, the reason is not measured).

### 56.2 Probe shapes

All new probes keep the note 55 readback: `store rt1, r0, r1` puts code(r0) in
byte 1 and code(r3/r1-style second operand) in byte 3, with
`code = 0xF7 + 8*log2(value)` for positive values, `00` for zero and negative
values, `FF` at 2.0 and above, and `78` as the smallest reportable code (about
2^-15.875). `s56_codes` re-calibrated that table down to the floor with twelve
runs and confirmed it (2^-15 -> `7F`, 2^-16 -> `78`, so 2^-16 and below all read
`78`; 0.0 and -1.0 both read `00`).

Four templates, all in `tests/grate/asm/`:

| template | shape |
|:--|:--|
| `mfu_s56_read.in` | seeds rows 0..3 from uniforms 5..8, copies them to shadow rows 4..7 in their own batch, one MFU word, zero or more intervening MFU words, an ALU read batch (plus optional second batch and extra batches), store |
| `mfu_s56_codes.in` | stores two constants, for the code table |
| `mfu_s56_rows.in` | seeds all sixteen rows from sixteen distinct values, one MFU word with a capture into r3, read r3 |
| `mfu_s56_idx.in` | like `mfu_s56_rows.in` but every row value comes from the manifest |

Two details matter for reading the numbers below. First, the seed vector is
written from uniforms 5..8 and uniforms 0..4 hold fixed distinct values
(8.0, 0.125, 3.0, 0.75, 1.0), so a row never holds the value of the uniform with
the same number; the earlier pilot used uniforms 0..3 as seeds and could not tell
index 0 from a source that reads uniform 0. Second, the plain read clips a 4.0
seed at `FF` and cannot show a negative at all, so the sign-and-axis work uses a
0.25-scaled read (`MAD r0.lh, rN, u9, #0`, u9 = 0.25) plus a negated variant of it.

### 56.3 The selector screen

Every value 0..63 of the operand field, three vectors with the same maximum
magnitude and the dominant axis moving (X4 = 4, 2, 1, 0.5; Y4 = 2, 4, 1, 0.5;
Z4 = 2, 1, 4, 0.5; `1/M` = 0.25 = `E7`). The result is the sfu output captured
through `mul0: r3, sfu, #1`, so the measurement is the multiplier's copy of the
sfu result and never a row that the operation may have written. Three repeats
each; the two standard controls before and after every chunk.

| residue | X4 | Y4 | Z4 |
|---:|:--|:--|:--|
| 0 | `E7` | `EF` | `EF` |
| 1 | `EF` | `E7` | `F7` |
| 2 | `F7` | `F7` | `E7` |
| 3 | `FF` | `FF` | `FF` |
| 4 | `78` | `78` | `78` |
| 5 | `78` | `78` | `00` |
| 6 | `00` | `00` | `78` |
| 7 | `00` | `00` | `00` |
| 8..15 | `E7` | `E7` | `E7` |

(The same word at `+16`, `+32` and `+48`; "residue" is `reg & 15`.)

Reading it:

- `0..3`: `1/r0`, `1/r1`, `1/r2`, `1/r3` -- X4 gives 0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0 for
  those seeds and Y4/Z4 track the seeds as they move. Ordinary reciprocal.
- `4..7`: constant across the three axes (`78`; `78/78/00`; `00/00/78`;
  `00/00/00`), so not a function of the dominant axis. *(Refined by 57.7: 5 and
  6 are not axis-invariant -- `sel5-Z4` and `sel6-Z4` are the cells that move,
  and 57.7 traces them to rows 1 and 2.)* `s56_idx` shows they do not
  change when the matching row is set to 0.5 or 4.0 either.
- `8..15`: 0.25 for all three axes, i.e. `1/M`, and unchanged when every row is
  1.0 (`idx`: `F7` = 1.0 for both content tests), so the operand is ignored.

`s56_scale` repeated 8..15 with M = 2, 4 and 8 on all three axes and with a
negative dominant component: every cell followed `1/M` (`EF`, `E7`, `DF`) and
stayed positive for negative dominant components, exactly as note 55 recorded
for 10. The `rcp r0` control in the same manifest repeats the same codes -- in
those vectors r0 *is* the dominant component -- so the discriminating checks are
the axis permutations, where a row operand would change code as the axis moves,
and the all-rows-1.0 table of 56.4, where a row operand would return 1.0 rather
than `1/M`.

One caution about reading these codes: the code is eight steps per octave and
truncates, so the reciprocal of a non-power-of-two lands up to a step low (1/0.75
reads `F9`, one step below the exact 1.333, and 1/1.25 reads `F3`). Every claim
below compares codes measured in the same run rather than codes against a
computed value, and the 0.25-scaled reads move the values into a range where a
step is easy to see.

### 56.4 What each residue reads

`s56_map` seeds rows 0..15 with 2.0, 1.0, 0.75, 1.25, 1.5, 2.5, 3.0, 4.0, 5.0,
6.0, 8.0, 10.0, 12.0, 16.0, 20.0 and 24.0, runs one opcode/index, and captures
through `mul0`. The interesting part is that the eight codes of indices 8..15 are
*byte-identical* to those of 0..7 for `rsq` and for `frc`:

| index | `rsq` | `frc` | `rcp` |
|---:|:--|:--|:--|
| 0 | `F2FF` (1/sqrt 2.0) | `00` (frac 2.0) | `EF` (1/2.0) |
| 1 | `F700` (1/sqrt 1.0) | `00` (frac 1.0) | `F7` (1/1.0) |
| 2 | `F8F2` (1/sqrt 0.75) | `F3` (frac 0.75) | `F9` (1/0.75) |
| 3 | `F5FF` (1/sqrt 1.25) | `E7` (frac 1.25) | `F3` (1/1.25) |
| 4 | `B700` | `00` | `78` |
| 5 | `7800` | `00` | `00` |
| 6 | `7541` | `00` | `00` |
| 7 | `76FF` | `00` | `00` |
| 8..15 | same as 0..7 | same as 0..7 | `EF` for all eight |

The `frc` column of 4..7 reads `00`, i.e. those sources are integral-valued; the
`rsq` column shows their reciprocals of the square root are between 2^-16 and
2^-8, so the sources are large (or, for 5..7, negative). They are not connected to
the sixteen-row table: `s56_idx` sets one row at a time to 0.5 and then to 4.0
with all others at 1.0 and gets the same code for both in every case.

`s56_idx` also confirms the positive side: indices 0..3 give `FF`/`E7` for
0.5/4.0, and 8..15 give `F7` (1/M of the untouched 1.0 rows) for both.

### 56.5 Multiplier sources in a normalization word

`mulmap` ran all six multiplier sources under each of 8..15 with three vectors;
`mulmap2` repeated the row sources with M = 2 and M = 8; `mulmap3` scaled the
capture by 0.25 in the reading batch so a value at or above 2.0 becomes
resolvable. The seeds are the row vector; row 1 and row 3 are read as seeded,
but row 0 and row 2 are not:

| source | index 10 | index 8 / 9 / 11..15 |
|:--|:--|:--|
| `r0` | the *written* row 0, i.e. the negated operand (-0.5 for a 0.5 row-2 seed) | row 2's pre-word value (0.5 for X2 and X8, 4.0 for the z vector) |
| `r1` | row 1's pre-word value | row 1's pre-word value |
| `r2` | **`1/M`** (0.5, 0.25, 0.125 for M = 2, 4, 8) | row 0's pre-word value (2.0 for X2, 8.0 for X8) |
| `r3` | row 3's pre-word value | row 3's pre-word value |
| `sfu` | `1/M` | `1/M` |
| `#1` | 1.0 | 1.0 |

So the source substitution note 55 found on row 2 is real and is *specific to
index 10*; the other seven members of the family route the row-0 and row-2
sources across each other instead. This is the one place where the eight values
are not interchangeable, and it is the reason the compiler's word works:
`mul0: r0, r2, r0` / `mul1: r1, r2, r1` with `rcp r10` gets `1/M` from the `r2`
field in both multiplier slots.

*(Corrected by 57.5: there is no crossed row port. Slot `r2` carries `M` and
slot `reg & 7` carries the sfu result (`1/M` for `rcp`), with the result
winning at slot 2, i.e. selector 10. The vectors used here all had `M = |r0|`
and row 2 = `1/M`, so the two readings coincided; the raw `X8` capture in this
note's own data is `DF` (0.125), i.e. `1/M`, not the 0.5 the table lists for it.)*

### 56.6 Other opcodes

`s56_ops` ran `rsq`, `sqrt`, `lg2` and `frc` on indices 8..15 with the same axis
permutations, plus ordinary row operands as controls. For every opcode, index 10
behaves exactly like index 2, and 11 like 3, 12 like 4, 13 like 5, 14 like 6 and
15 like 7:

| probe | result | reads |
|:--|:--|:--|
| `rsq r10` on X2/Y2/Z2 (row 2 = 0.5, 0.5, 2.0) | `FAFF`/`FAFF`/`F2FF` | 1/sqrt(row 2): 1.414, 1.414, 0.707 |
| `rsq r11` on X2 (row 3 = 0.25) | `FF` | 1/sqrt(0.25) = 2.0 |
| `sqrt r10` on X2/Z2 (row 2 = 0.5, 2.0) | `F2FF` / `FAFF` | sqrt(0.5), sqrt(2.0) |
| `lg2 r10` on X4/Y4/Z4 (row 2 = 1, 1, 4) | `00`/`00`/`FF` | 0, 0, 2 |
| `frc r10` on fX/fY/fZ (row 2 = 0.25, 0.25, 1.125) | `E7`/`E7`/`DF` | 0.25, 0.25, 0.125 |

**Confirmed:** the major-axis operand is an `rcp`-only interpretation. Feeding
index 10 to `rsq`, `sqrt`, `lg2` or `frc` gives the ordinary operation applied to
row 2, not `1/M` and not anything derived from the dominant axis. So the
hardware does not "make index 10 mean the maximum" for all SFU operations; it
has a normalizing reciprocal that the same field selects, and the other
single-step operations ignore bit 3 entirely.

Paired operations (`PREEX2`/`EX2`, `PRESIN`/`SIN`, `PRECOS`/`COS`) were not run:
their two-word setup is not implemented in these probes and a wrong pairing would
produce a meaningless result rather than a bounded one.

### 56.7 The row writes: a descriptor and a negated operand

Rows 0..2 are written by a normalization-family `rcp`. With
`d = argmax(|r0|,|r1|,|r2|)` (ties go to the highest index) and `s = sign(r_d)`:

1. **row 2 <- the descriptor** `2d + (s < 0)`, as a floating-point number 0..5.
2. **if d is 0 or 1: row d <- -s * (row 2 before the word)**, and the other of
   rows 0/1 keeps its value.
3. **if d is 2: row 0 <- -(row 0 before the word)**, and row 1 keeps its
   value. *(Corrected by 57.3: row 0 is multiplied by the **sign of row 2** --
   unchanged for a positive dominant Z, negated for a negative one. The
   measurement matrix below is right; this prose rule was not.)*
4. rows 3..15 keep their values.

Measured matrix, maximum 4.0, using the 0.25-scaled and the negated-scaled read
(three repeats, identical; `q`/`qn` are the two raw store words of the second
lane unless noted):

| vector r0,r1,r2 | row 0 | row 1 | row 2 | d, s |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| 4, 2, 0.5 | -0.5 (`00`/`DF`) | 2.0 (`EF`) | 0 (`00`) | 0, + |
| -4, 2, 0.5 | +0.5 (`DF`) | 2.0 | 1 (`E7`) | 0, - |
| 2, 4, 0.5 | 2.0 | -0.5 (`00`/`DF`) | 2 (`EF`) | 1, + |
| 2, -4, 0.5 | 2.0 | +0.5 (`DF`) | 3 (`F3`) | 1, - |
| 2, 0.5, 4 | 2.0 | 0.5 | 4 (`F7`) | 2, + |
| 2, 0.5, -4 | -2.0 (`00`/`EF`) | 0.5 | 5 (`F9`) | 2, - |
| 4, -4, 0.5 | 4.0 (`F7`) | +0.5 (`DF`) | 3 (`F3`) | 1, - (tie 0/1) |
| 4, 0.5, -4 | -4.0 (`00`/`F7`) | 0.5 | 5 | 2, - (tie 0/2) |
| 0.5, 4, -4 | -0.5 (`00`/`DF`) | 4.0 | 5 | 2, - (tie 1/2) |
| -4, -4, -4 | +4.0 (`F7`) | -4.0 (`00`/`F7`) | 5 | 2, - (three-way tie) |

(`row 3` read 1.0 -- its seed -- in every cell of the matrix.)

The rule was checked against 24 more vectors with distinct seed magnitudes and
signs (`sweep`, 30 vectors; `sign2`, 13; `sign3`, 14), including a case where row
2 itself is negative (the negated operand then has a plus sign: r2 = -0.75 with a
positive dominant row 1 gives row 1 = +0.75). Two independent readings agree on
each cell: the multiplier's copy of a written row (56.5, `r0` column for index
10) and the ALU copy, with `abs()` and the negated read supplying the sign that
the plain read cannot show.

**Inferred, not measured:** the descriptor's two parts (`2d` and the sign bit) and
the negated operand look like staging for a normalizing lowering -- the
descriptor is exactly what a face-selection step would need, and `-s * coordinate`
is the operand whose reciprocal has the dominant sign. Nothing here shows what
consumes them; a shader that does not read rows 0..2 after the word does not care.

**Edge case, unresolved:** with all three coordinate rows at 0.0 the descriptor
reads 4 (0.5 via the 0.25 read is impossible here; the raw code is `F7`), i.e. it
reports axis 2 positive for a degenerate vector, and rows 0/1 read 0. One run,
three repeats.

### 56.8 Read paths, signs and delays

`s56_path` calibrated eleven read forms on unmodified rows (word `nop r0`) and
then applied them to the written rows for the four sign/axis combinations. All
forms agree; the differences are exactly the ones the store format forces:

| form | reads | positive-x case, row 0 |
|:--|:--|:--|
| `MAD r0.lh, rN, #1, #0` (plain) | the row, clipped at 2.0, `00` for <= 0 | `00` |
| `abs(rN)` then copy | magnitude | `EF` (0.5) |
| `-rN` then copy | negated; `00` unless the row was negative | `EF` (0.5) |
| times 0.25 from uniform 9 | four times the resolution | `00` |
| `abs(rN)` times 0.25 | magnitude, scaled | `DF` (0.125) |
| `-rN` times 0.25 | negated, scaled | `DF` (0.125) |
| times 1.0 from uniform 4 | the row through a real multiply | `00` |
| copy into r8, then copy r8 (two batches) | the row, two steps later | `00` |
| `abs` into r8, then copy | magnitude, two steps later | `EF` |
| row as the rC operand instead of rB | the row | `00` |

So the note 55 "`abs()` contradiction" is explained by the sign, not by a read
path: the row holds -0.5, the plain read stores `00` (negative), `abs()` stores
0.5. `abs()` is faithful on ordinary rows in the same manifest, and the rB/rC
positions agree, which is why the earlier note could not reconcile them.

`s56_reread` asked the write-versus-read question directly: after the word, a
second MFU word read the row through its sfu (`rcp r0`, captured through the
multiplier) and through a multiplier source (`mul0: r3, r0, #1`); both agree with
the ALU copy, and the values are unchanged after one and two empty ALU batches.
`s56_delay` repeated the four sign/axis cases with zero, one and two intervening
MFU NOP words and with and without a later batch boundary: identical in all 12
combinations per case, while the shadow row 4 stayed at its seed in every one.
**Confirmed:** the writes are register contents, not a read-port or store-path
effect, and they do not decay.

### 56.9 Multiplier shapes and the compiler's word

`s56_shape` ran six multiplier shapes over the ten-vector matrix, reading rows
0..3 with the 0.25 path after the word. For the positive-x vector (4, 2, 0.5, 1,
M = 4, 1/M = 0.25):

| shape | row 0 | row 1 | row 2 | row 3 |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| `rcp r10`, no multipliers | `00` (-0.5) | `EF` (2.0) | `00` (0) | `E7` (1.0) |
| `mul0: r3, sfu, #1` | `00` | `EF` | `00` | `D7` (0.25) |
| `mul1: r3, sfu, #1` | `00` | `EF` | `00` | `D7` (0.25) |
| `mul0: r1, sfu, #1` | `00` | `D7` (0.25) | `00` | `E7` (1.0) |
| `mul1: r0, sfu, #1` | `D7` (0.25) | `EF` | `00` | `E7` |
| compiler: `mul0: r0, r2, r0`, `mul1: r1, r2, r1` | `00` | `DF` (0.5) | `00` | `E7` |

Two things follow. First, activating a multiplier does not change the descriptor
or the negated operand: the row writes of the sfu part are the same in every
shape, and the multiplier only adds its own destination. Second, the compiler's
word computes `r1 = 1/M * r1 = 0.5` correctly but its `r0` lane is the product of
`1/M` with the *written* row 0 (-0.5), i.e. -0.125, not with x. For a fragment
where x is the dominant coordinate this makes the r0 lane zero or negative --
which is what note 55 measured as `0xD7000000`, and what a real cube-normalizing
shader would have to compensate for by not relying on the r0 product.

### 56.10 What this note establishes, and what it does not

Established by measurement: the operand-field decode (bits 5:4 ignored; low
nibble 0..3 rows, 4..7 internal, 8..15 normalization); that 8..15 are the
normalization family for `rcp` and only `rcp`; that the family writes a
descriptor into row 2 and a negated operand into one coordinate row by the rule
above; that the family's row 2 is the only multiplier source that carries `1/M`
and only for index 10; that ordinary `rcp` writes its result back into the row it
reads; that the writes are stable; and that the note 55 `abs()` discrepancy was a
sign effect.

Not established: the mechanism behind the writes; what low nibble 4..7 are; why
the multiplier's row-0 and row-2 operands are crossed for indices other than 10;
what the descriptor is consumed by; and anything about face selection, which
remains blocked (56.11).

Practical rules for a shader: `rcp r10` through `rcp r15` are interchangeable if
the reciprocal is taken from the `sfu` multiplier source; if a multiplier must
read the reciprocal from the `r2` field, use index 10; do not read rows 0..2
after the word; do not expect the multiplier's `r0` field to hold the coordinate
it named.

### 56.11 Cube / TEX reconnaissance

Bounded, and it found nothing new:

- The proprietary compiler is still absent: `/home/freebuff/build/grate-exp/tools/cgc`
  prints `CgDrv_Create: BLOB compiler is unavailable` and falls back to reading
  stdin; there is no `libcgdrv.so` in `/usr/lib` or
  `/usr/lib/arm-linux-gnueabihf`. `tools/cgc` is the `dummy.c` fallback.
- No raw cube shader dump exists. `git log --all -S'textureCube'` matches only
  the note-55 commit, and the only files mentioning a cube map in the checkout are
  the notes themselves. `tests/grate/cube-textured*.c` and `cube.c` are textured
  *cube* demos: they bind one 2D surface (`data/tegra.png`), and their shader files
  (`tests/grate/asm/cube2_fs.txt` and friends) do not exist in the tree.
- The TEX instruction has no dimension or face field:
  `tex_instr` is `sampler_index:4, src_regs_select, sample_dst_regs_select,
  unk_6_9:4, enable, unk_11, enable_bias, explicit_lod, unk_14_31:18`, and the
  disassembler prints only the sampler index, the coordinate source pair
  (`r0..r3` or `r2, r3, r0`) and the destination pair. There is nowhere in the
  instruction for a face or target selector, so any face selection would have to
  live in sampler or target state.
- The sampler-descriptor investigation recorded in
  `docs_snap/fragment-shader-isa.md` (commit `0ff925b`) stands: `TEXTURE_DESC1`
  and `TEXTURE_DESC2` have no dimension or face field, the driver allocates one
  2D surface, and the 32-bit descriptor sweep plus the R-sweep found no field
  that enables face selection. That is a statement about the harness's 2D
  binding, not about the hardware, and this note adds nothing to it.

Conclusion: face selection and face-coordinate orientation remain **unresolved**,
blocked on a compiler that can emit a cube shader and on a binding path for a
cube target. Neither was created here. Mesa's normalizing lowerings remain an
algorithmic reference only.

### 56.12 Runs and method notes

Device: Toshiba AC100, Tegra 20 / AR20, `armv7l`, kernel
`7.2.0-gd1efa725955b`. Repository `i-am-cipher-null/grate`, branch
`code/fragment-isa-experiments`, head
`a7775c453522f6f6de201199c2cc463d2e76b4d3` at the first manifest (equal to
`github/code/fragment-isa-experiments`; the docs branch was left alone at
`0ff925b`).

(`immediate-operand` expecting `0x000000FF`, `varying-pipeline` expecting
`0xFF01FF00`) run once before and once after the variants. Run data is under the
